<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 660 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 280 s 9</a>-14,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 567 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 527 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 22 s 8</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/277/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 277 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/630/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 630 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 212 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 194 s 2 </a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.19.64.0">2023 c 52 art 19 s 64</a>-67</p></div> <p><b>NOTE:</b> Any statutory exemptions to this section are covered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.23">363A.23</a>.</p><div class="section" id="stat.363A.14"><h1 class="shn">363A.14 AIDING AND ABETTING AND OBSTRUCTION.</h1><p>It is an unfair discriminatory practice for any person:</p><p>(1) intentionally to aid, abet, incite, compel, or coerce a person to engage in any of the practices forbidden by this chapter;</p><p>(2) intentionally to attempt to aid, abet, incite, compel, or coerce a person to engage in any of the practices forbidden by this chapter;</p><p>(3) to intentionally obstruct or prevent any person from complying with the provisions of this chapter, or any order issued thereunder, or to resist, prevent, impede, or interfere with the commissioner or any of the commissioner's employees or representatives in the performance of duty under this chapter.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.14.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.5.0">1955 c 516 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.5.0">1961 c 428 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 729 s 3</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/354/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 354 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/206/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 206 s 2</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.59.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.7.0">1983 c 276 s 7</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/533/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 533 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 23 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 129 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/141/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 141 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/245/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 245 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 660 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 280 s 9</a>-14,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 567 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 527 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 22 s 8</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/277/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 277 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/630/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 630 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 212 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 194 s 2</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.15"><h1 class="shn">363A.15 REPRISALS.</h1><p>It is an unfair discriminatory practice for any individual who participated in the alleged discrimination as a perpetrator, employer, labor organization, employment agency, public accommodation, public service, educational institution, or owner, lessor, lessee, sublessee, assignee or managing agent of any real property, or any real estate broker, real estate salesperson, or employee or agent thereof to intentionally engage in any reprisal against any person because that person:</p><p>(1) opposed a practice forbidden under this chapter or has filed a charge, testified, assisted, or participated in any manner in an investigation, proceeding, or hearing under this chapter; or</p><p>(2) associated with a person or group of persons who are disabled or who are of different race, color, creed, religion, gender identity, sexual orientation, or national origin.</p><p>A reprisal includes, but is not limited to, any form of intimidation, retaliation, or harassment. It is a reprisal for an employer to do any of the following with respect to an individual because that individual has engaged in the activities listed in clause (1) or (2): refuse to hire the individual; depart from any customary employment practice; transfer or assign the individual to a lesser position in terms of wages, hours, job classification, job security, or other employment status; or inform another employer that the individual has engaged in the activities listed in clause (1) or (2).</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.15.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.5.0">1955 c 516 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.5.0">1961 c 428 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/585/#laws.0.2.0">1965 c 585 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/586/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 586 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.12.0">1967 c 897 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 729 s 3</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/354/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 354 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/206/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 206 s 2</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/540/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 540 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/330/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 330 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/517/#laws.0.8.0">1982 c 517 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.59.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.7.0">1983 c 276 s 7</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/533/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 533 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 23 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 129 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/141/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 141 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/245/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 245 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 660 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 280 s 9</a>-14,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 567 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 527 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 22 s 8</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/277/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 277 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/630/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 630 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 212 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 194 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.19.68.0">2023 c 52 art 19 s 68</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.16"><h1 class="shn">363A.16 CREDIT DISCRIMINATION.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.16.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.16.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Personal or commercial credit.</span></h2><p>It is an unfair discriminatory practice to discriminate in the extension of personal or commercial credit to a person, or in the requirements for obtaining credit, because of race, color, creed, religion, disability, national origin, sex, gender identity, sexual orientation, familial status, or marital status, or due to the receipt of federal, state, or local public assistance including medical assistance.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.16.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.16.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Personal or commercial credit; tenant on assistance.</span></h2><p>It is an unfair discriminatory practice to discriminate in the extension of personal or commercial credit against any person who is a tenant receiving federal, state, or local housing subsidies, including rental assistance or rent supplements because the person is a recipient of those subsidies or assistance.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.16.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.16.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Credit card issuer.</span></h2><p>It is an unfair discriminatory practice for a credit card issuer to refuse to issue a credit card to a woman under her current or former surname unless there is an intent to defraud or mislead, except that a credit card issuer may require that a woman requesting a card under a former surname open a separate account in that name. A credit card issuer may also require disclosure of any other names under which the credit card applicant may have a credit history.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.16.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.5.0">1955 c 516 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.5.0">1961 c 428 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 23 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 660 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 280 s 9</a>-14,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 567 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 527 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 22 s 8</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/277/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 277 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/630/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 630 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 212 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 194 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.19.69.0">2023 c 52 art 19 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/105/#laws.0.9.0">2024 c 105 s 9</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.17"><h1 class="shn">363A.17 BUSINESS DISCRIMINATION.</h1><p>It is an unfair discriminatory practice for a person engaged in a trade or business or in the provision of a service:</p><p>(1) to refuse to do business with or provide a service to a woman based on her use of her current or former surname; or</p><p>(2) to impose, as a condition of doing business with or providing a service to a woman, that a woman use her current surname rather than a former surname; or</p><p>(3) to intentionally refuse to do business with, to refuse to contract with, or to discriminate in the basic terms, conditions, or performance of the contract because of a person's race, national origin, color, sex, gender identity, sexual orientation, or disability, unless the alleged refusal or discrimination is because of a legitimate business purpose.</p><p>Nothing in this section shall prohibit positive action plans.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.17.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.5.0">1955 c 516 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.5.0">1961 c 428 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/585/#laws.0.2.0">1965 c 585 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/586/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 586 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.12.0">1967 c 897 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 729 s 3</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/354/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 354 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/206/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 206 s 2</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/351/#laws.0.5.0">1977 c 351 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/408/#laws.0.3.0">1977 c 408 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/531/#laws.0.4.0">1980 c 531 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/540/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 540 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/330/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 330 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/517/#laws.0.8.0">1982 c 517 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.59.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.7.0">1983 c 276 s 7</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/533/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 533 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 23 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 129 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/141/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 141 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/245/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 245 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 660 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 280 s 9</a>-14,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 567 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 527 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 22 s 8</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/277/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 277 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/630/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 630 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 212 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 194 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.19.70.0">2023 c 52 art 19 s 70</a></p></div><p><b>NOTE: </b>Causes of action under this section which require interpretation of a federal labor agreement were found preempted by the federal National Labor Relations Act in <i>Boldt v. Northern States Power Co.</i>, 195 F.Supp.3d 1057 (D. Minn. 2016).</p><div class="sr" id="stat.363A.18"><b>363A.18</b> [Reserved]</div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.19"><h1 class="shn">363A.19 DISCRIMINATION AGAINST PERSONS WITH DISABILITIES PROHIBITED.</h1><p>(a) It is an unfair discriminatory practice for an owner, operator, or manager of a hotel, restaurant, public conveyance, or other public place to prohibit a person with a disability from taking a service animal into the public place or conveyance to aid persons with disabilities, and if the service animal is properly harnessed or leashed so that the person with a disability may maintain control of the service animal.</p><p>(b) No person shall require a person with a disability to make an extra payment or pay an additional charge when taking a service animal into any of the public places referred to in paragraph (a).</p><p>(c) For purposes of this section, "service animal" means a service animal as defined by the federal Americans with Disabilities Act, as amended.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.19.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.5.0">1955 c 516 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.5.0">1961 c 428 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/585/#laws.0.2.0">1965 c 585 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/586/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 586 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.12.0">1967 c 897 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/9/#laws.0.80.0">1969 c 9 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.7.0">1983 c 276 s 7</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/533/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 533 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 23 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 129 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/141/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 141 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/245/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 245 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 660 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 280 s 9</a>-14,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 567 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 527 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 22 s 8</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/277/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 277 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/630/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 630 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 212 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 194 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/14/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 14 s 1 </a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/105/#laws.0.10.0">2024 c 105 s 10</a></p></div> <p><b>NOTE:</b> Any statutory exemptions to this section are covered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.25">363A.25</a>.</p><h1 class="fgh">EXEMPTIONS TO UNFAIR DISCRIMINATORY PRACTICES</h1><div class="section" id="stat.363A.20"><h1 class="shn">363A.20 EXEMPTION BASED ON EMPLOYMENT.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Employment.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> shall not apply to the employment of any individual:</p><p>(1) by the individual's parent, grandparent, spouse, child, or grandchild; or</p><p>(2) in the domestic service of any person.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote"> Fraternal organization.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> shall not apply to a fraternal corporation, association, or society, with respect to qualifications based on religion, when religion is a bona fide occupational qualification for employment.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2><p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.19.72.0">2023 c 52 art 19 s 72</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Employment selection.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> do not apply to the employment of one person in place of another, standing by itself, shall not be evidence of an unfair discriminatory practice.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.5"><a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.5">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Seniority system.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> do not apply to the operation of a bona fide seniority system which mandates differences in such things as wages, hiring priorities, layoff priorities, vacation credit, and job assignments based on seniority, so long as the operation of the system is not a subterfuge to evade the provisions of this chapter.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.6"><a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.6">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Insurance, fringe benefits, and age requirements.</span></h2><p>(a) With respect to age discrimination, the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> do not apply to a practice by which a labor organization or employer offers or supplies varying insurance benefits or other fringe benefits to members or employees of differing ages, so long as the cost to the labor organization or employer for the benefits is reasonably equivalent for all members or employees.</p><p>(b) Nothing in this chapter concerning age discrimination shall be construed to validate or permit age requirements which have a disproportionate impact on persons of any class otherwise protected by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.13">363A.13</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.7"><a title="Link to Subd. 7." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.7">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Peace officer or firefighter.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> do not apply to a restriction imposed by state statute, home rule charter, ordinance, or civil service rule, and applied uniformly and without exception to all individuals, which establishes a maximum age for entry into employment as a peace officer or firefighter.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.8"><a title="Link to Subd. 8." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.8">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Physical exam.</span></h2><p>(a) It is not an unfair employment practice for an employer, employment agency, or labor organization:</p><p>(1) to require or request a person to undergo physical examination, which may include a medical history, for the purpose of determining the person's capability to perform available employment, provided:</p><p>(i) that an offer of employment has been made on condition that the person meets the physical or mental requirements of the job, except that a law enforcement agency filling a peace officer position or part-time peace officer position may require or request an applicant to undergo psychological evaluation before a job offer is made provided that the psychological evaluation is for those job-related abilities set forth by the Board of Peace Officer Standards and Training for psychological evaluations and is otherwise lawful;</p><p>(ii) that the examination tests only for essential job-related abilities;</p><p>(iii) that the examination except for examinations authorized under chapter 176 is required of all persons conditionally offered employment for the same position regardless of disability; and</p><p>(iv) that the information obtained regarding the medical condition or history of the applicant is collected and maintained on separate forms and in separate medical files and is treated as a confidential medical record, except that supervisors and managers may be informed regarding necessary restrictions on the work or duties of the employee and necessary accommodations; first aid safety personnel may be informed, when appropriate, if the disability might require emergency treatment; government officials investigating compliance with this chapter must be provided relevant information on request; and information may be released for purposes mandated by local, state, or federal law; provided that the results of the examination are used only in accordance with this chapter;</p><p>(2) with the consent of the employee, after employment has commenced, to obtain additional medical information for the purposes of assessing continuing ability to perform the job or employee health insurance eligibility; for purposes mandated by local, state, or federal law; for purposes of assessing the need to reasonably accommodate an employee or obtaining information to determine eligibility for the second injury fund under chapter 176; or pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/181.950">181.950</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/181.957">181.957</a>; or other legitimate business reason not otherwise prohibited by law;</p><p>(3) to administer preemployment tests, provided that the tests: (i) measure only essential job-related abilities; (ii) are required of all applicants for the same position regardless of disability, except for tests authorized under chapter 176; and (iii) accurately measure the applicant's aptitude, achievement level, or whatever factors they purport to measure rather than reflecting the applicant's impaired sensory, manual, or speaking skills except when those skills are the factors that the tests purport to measure;</p><p>(4) to limit receipt of benefits payable under a fringe benefit plan for disabilities to that period of time which a licensed physician reasonably determines a person is unable to work; or</p><p>(5) to provide special safety considerations for pregnant women involved in tasks which are potentially hazardous to the health of the unborn child, as determined by medical criteria.</p><p>(b) Information obtained under paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (5), subdivisions 1 to 7, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.21">363A.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.26">363A.26</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.38">363A.38</a>, regarding the medical condition or history of any employee, is subject to the requirements of paragraph (a), clause (1)(iv).</p><p>(c) If any health care records or medical information adversely affects any hiring, firing, or promotional decision concerning an applicant or employee, the employer must notify the affected party of that information within ten days of the final decision.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.9"><a title="Link to Subd. 9." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.9">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Mandatory retirement age.</span></h2><p>By law or published retirement policy, a mandatory retirement age may be established without being a violation of this chapter if it is established consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/181.81">181.81</a>. Nothing in this chapter nor in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/181.81">181.81</a> shall prohibit employee pension and retirement plans from granting pension credit to employees over the age of 65 at a lesser rate than is granted to other employees, provided that in no event may an employee's accumulated pension credits be reduced by continued employment, and further provided that no other state or federal law is violated by the reduced rate of pension credit accrual. Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to prohibit the establishment of differential privileges, benefits, services, or facilities for persons of designated ages if (1) such differential treatment is provided pursuant to statute, or (2) the designated age is greater than 59 years or less than 21 years. Clause (1) does not apply to hiring, tenure, compensation, upgrading, or conditions of employment.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.20.10"><a title="Link to Subd. 10." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.20.10">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Summer youth employment program.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> with regard to age shall not apply to the state summer youth employment program administered by the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.20.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/584/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 584 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.10.0">1967 c 897 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 729 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/206/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 206 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/351/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 351 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/408/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 408 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/430/#laws.0.25.0">1977 c 430 s 25</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/649/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 649 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/355/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 355 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/608/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 608 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 129 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 660 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/105/#laws.0.11.0">2024 c 105 s 11</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.21"><h1 class="shn">363A.21 EXEMPTION BASED ON REAL PROPERTY.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.21.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.21.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Housing.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.09">363A.09</a> shall not apply to:</p><p>(1) rooms in a temporary or permanent residence home run by a nonprofit organization, if the discrimination is by sex; or</p><p>(2) the rental by a resident owner or occupier of a one-family accommodation of a room or rooms in the accommodation to another person or persons if the discrimination is by sex, gender identity, marital status, status with regard to public assistance, sexual orientation, or disability. Except as provided elsewhere in this chapter or other state or federal law, no person or group of persons selling, renting, or leasing property is required to modify the property in any way, or exercise a higher degree of care for a person having a disability than for a person who does not have a disability; nor shall this chapter be construed to relieve any person or persons of any obligations generally imposed on all persons regardless of any disability in a written lease, rental agreement, or contract of purchase or sale, or to forbid distinctions based on the inability to fulfill the terms and conditions, including financial obligations of the lease, agreement, or contract.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.21.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.21.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Familial status.</span></h2><p>(a) The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.09">363A.09</a> prohibiting discrimination because of familial status shall not be construed to defeat the applicability of any local, state, or federal restrictions regarding the maximum number of occupants permitted to occupy a dwelling unit and shall not apply to any owner-occupied building containing four or fewer dwelling units or housing for elderly persons.</p><p>(b) "Housing for elderly persons" means housing:</p><p>(1) provided under any state or federal program that the commissioner determines is specifically designed and operated to assist elderly persons, as defined in the state or federal program;</p><p>(2) intended for, and solely occupied by, persons 62 years of age or older; or</p><p>(3) intended and operated for occupancy by at least one person 55 years of age or older per unit, provided that at least 80 percent of the units are occupied by at least one person 55 years of age or older per unit, and there is publication of, and adherence to, policies and procedures that demonstrate an intent by the owner or manager to provide housing for persons 55 years of age or older.</p><p>(c) Housing does not fail to meet the requirements for housing for elderly persons by reason of persons residing in the housing as of August 1, 1989, who do not meet the age requirements of paragraph (b), clauses (2) and (3), if new occupants of the housing meet the age requirements of paragraph (b), clause (2) or (3). In addition, housing does not fail to meet the requirements by reason of unoccupied units if unoccupied units are reserved for occupancy by persons who meet the age requirements of paragraph (b), clause (2) or (3).</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.21.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.21.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Age.</span></h2><p>Notwithstanding the provisions of any law, ordinance, or home rule charter to the contrary, no person shall be deemed to have committed an unfair discriminatory practice based upon age if the unfair discriminatory practice alleged is attempted or accomplished for the purpose of obtaining or maintaining one of the exemptions provided for a dwelling unit provided for in this section.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.21.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/584/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 584 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.10.0">1967 c 897 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 729 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/608/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 608 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.19.71.0">2023 c 52 art 19 s 71</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.22"><h1 class="shn">363A.22 EXEMPTION BASED ON FAMILIAL STATUS IN HOUSING.</h1><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.09">363A.09</a> prohibiting discrimination because of familial status do not apply to eviction from, or denial of continuing tenancy in, dwelling units exempt under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.20">363A.20</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.26">363A.26</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.38">363A.38</a>, provided that: (1) one year has elapsed from the commencement of the familial status; and (2) six months prior written notice has been given to the tenant, unless the eviction or denial of continuing tenancy is for nonpayment of rent, damage to the premises, disturbance of other tenants, or other breach of the lease.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.22.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/584/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 584 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.10.0">1967 c 897 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 729 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/206/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 206 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/351/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 351 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/408/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 408 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/430/#laws.0.25.0">1977 c 430 s 25</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/649/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 649 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/355/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 355 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/608/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 608 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 129 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 660 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.23"><h1 class="shn">363A.23 EXEMPTION BASED ON EDUCATION.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.23.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.23.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Religious or denominational institution.</span></h2><p>It is not an unfair discriminatory practice for a religious or denominational institution to limit admission or give preference to applicants of the same religion. The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.13">363A.13</a> relating to sex, shall not apply to a private educational institution, or branch or level of a private educational institution, in which students of only one sex are permitted to enroll. Nothing in this chapter shall prohibit an educational institution from using academic qualifications or achievements as criteria for admission or requiring from applicants information which relates to academic qualifications or achievements.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.23.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.23.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Athletic teams.</span></h2><p>Notwithstanding any other provisions of this chapter or any law to the contrary, it is not an unfair discriminatory practice for an educational institution or a public service to operate or sponsor separate athletic teams and activities for members of each sex or to restrict membership on an athletic team to participants of one sex, if this separation or restriction meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.04">121A.04</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.23.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.23.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Process for investigating charges against some athletic programs.</span></h2><p>The Department of Human Rights shall investigate all charges alleging sex discrimination in athletic programs in educational institutions and public services pursuant to the standards and requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.04">121A.04</a> and the procedures enumerated in this chapter.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.23.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.23.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Other rights, procedures, and remedies.</span></h2><p>Nothing in this chapter restricts or limits the rights, procedures, and remedies available under section 504 of the Rehabilitation Act of 1973, United States Code, title 29, section 794, or the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, sections 1401 and following.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.23.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.24"><h1 class="shn">363A.24 EXEMPTION BASED ON PUBLIC ACCOMMODATIONS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.24.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.24.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Restrooms, locker rooms, and similar places.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.11">363A.11</a> relating to sex, shall not apply to such facilities as restrooms, locker rooms, and other similar places. The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.11">363A.11</a> do not apply to employees or volunteers of a nonpublic service organization whose primary function is providing occasional services to minors, such as youth sports organizations, scouting organizations, boys' or girls' clubs, programs providing friends, counselors, or role models for minors, youth theater, dance, music or artistic organizations, agricultural organizations for minors, and other youth organizations, with respect to qualifications based on sexual orientation.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.24.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.24.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Athletic team, program, or event.</span></h2><p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.11">363A.11</a> relating to sex, do not apply to restricting membership on an athletic team or in a program or event to participants of one sex if the restriction is necessary to preserve the unique character of the team, program, or event and it would not substantially reduce comparable athletic opportunities for the other sex.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.24.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/584/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 584 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.10.0">1967 c 897 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 729 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/206/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 206 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/351/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 351 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/408/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 408 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/430/#laws.0.25.0">1977 c 430 s 25</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/649/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 649 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/355/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 355 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/509/#laws.0.143.0">1980 c 509 s 143</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/531/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 531 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/492/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 492 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/526/#laws.2.16.0">1982 c 526 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 276 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.198.0">1983 c 301 s 198</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/608/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 608 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 129 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 660 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.25"><h1 class="shn">363A.25 EXEMPTION BASED ON DISABILITY.</h1><p>Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to prohibit any program, service, facility, or privilege afforded to a person with a disability which is intended to habilitate, rehabilitate, or accommodate that person. It is a defense to a complaint or action brought under the employment provisions of this chapter that the person bringing the complaint or action has a disability which in the circumstances and even with reasonable accommodation, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08#stat.363A.08.6">363A.08, subdivision 6</a>, poses a serious threat to the health or safety of the disabled person or others. The burden of proving this defense is upon the respondent.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.25.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/509/#laws.0.143.0">1980 c 509 s 143</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/531/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 531 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/492/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 492 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/526/#laws.2.16.0">1982 c 526 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 276 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.198.0">1983 c 301 s 198</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/608/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 608 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 129 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 660 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.26"><h1 class="shn">363A.26 EXEMPTION BASED ON RELIGIOUS ASSOCIATION.</h1><p>Nothing in this chapter prohibits any religious association, religious corporation, or religious society that is not organized for private profit, or any institution organized for educational purposes that is operated, supervised, or controlled by a religious association, religious corporation, or religious society that is not organized for private profit, consistent with the protections or privileges of the First Amendment of the United States Constitution and article I, section 16, of the Minnesota Constitution, including ministerial exceptions provided therein, from:</p><p>(1) limiting admission to or giving preference to persons of the same religion or denomination;</p><p>(2) taking any action with respect to education, employment, housing and real property, or use of facilities; or</p><p>(3) taking any action with respect to the provision of goods, services, facilities, or accommodations directly related to the solemnization or celebration of a civil marriage that is in violation of its religious beliefs.</p><p class="bl">This section shall not apply to secular business activities engaged in by the religious association, religious corporation, religious society, or any institution organized for educational purposes that is operated, supervised, or controlled by a religious association, religious corporation, or religious society that is not organized for private profit, the conduct of which is unrelated to the religious and educational purposes for which it is organized.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.26.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/430/#laws.0.25.0">1977 c 430 s 25</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/649/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 649 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/355/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 355 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/509/#laws.0.143.0">1980 c 509 s 143</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/531/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 531 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/492/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 492 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/526/#laws.2.16.0">1982 c 526 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 276 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.198.0">1983 c 301 s 198</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/608/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 608 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 129 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 660 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/74/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 74 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/105/#laws.0.12.0">2024 c 105 s 12</a></p></div><h1 class="fgh">LEGAL PROCEEDINGS AND ACTIONS</h1><div class="sr" id="stat.363A.27"><b>363A.27</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.19.72.0">2023 c 52 art 19 s 72</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.28"><h1 class="shn">363A.28 GRIEVANCES.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Actions.</span></h2><p>Any person aggrieved by a violation of this chapter may bring a civil action as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.33#stat.363A.33.1">363A.33, subdivision 1</a>, or may file a verified charge with the commissioner or the commissioner's designated agent. A charge filed with the commissioner must be in writing by hand, or electronically with an unsworn declaration under penalty of perjury, on a form provided by the commissioner and signed by the charging party. The charge must state the name of the person alleged to have committed an unfair discriminatory practice and set out a summary of the details of the practice complained of. The commissioner may require a charging party to provide the address of the person alleged to have committed the unfair discriminatory practice, names of witnesses, documents, and any other information necessary to process the charge. The commissioner may dismiss a charge when the charging party fails to provide required information. The commissioner within ten days of the filing shall serve a copy of the charge and a form for use in responding to the charge upon the respondent personally, electronically with the receiving party's consent, or by mail. The respondent shall file with the department a written response setting out a summary of the details of the respondent's position relative to the charge within 30 days of receipt of the charge. If the respondent fails to respond with a written summary of the details of the respondent's position within 30 days after service of the charge, and service was consistent with <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/cp/id/4/">rule 4</a> of the Rules of Civil Procedure, the commissioner, on behalf of the complaining party, may bring an action for default in district court pursuant to<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/cp/id/55/#55.01"> rule 55.01</a> of the Rules of Civil Procedure.</p><p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's charge.</span></h2><p>Whenever the commissioner has reason to believe that a person is engaging in an unfair discriminatory practice, the commissioner may issue a charge stating in statutory language an alleged violation of subdivision 10 and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.19">363A.19</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">For filing claim; filing options.</span></h2><p>(a) A claim of an unfair discriminatory practice must be brought as a civil action pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.33#stat.363A.33.1">363A.33, subdivision 1</a>, filed in a charge with a local commission pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.07#stat.363A.07.3">363A.07, subdivision 3</a>, or filed in a charge with the commissioner within one year after the occurrence of the practice.</p><p>(b) The running of the one-year limitation period is suspended during the time a potential charging party and respondent are voluntarily engaged in a dispute resolution process involving a claim of unlawful discrimination under this chapter, including arbitration, conciliation, mediation or grievance procedures pursuant to a collective bargaining agreement or statutory, charter, ordinance provisions for a civil service or other employment system or a school board sexual harassment or sexual violence policy. A potential respondent who participates in such a process with a potential charging party before a charge is filed or a civil action is brought shall notify the department and the charging party in writing of the participation in the process and the date the process commenced and shall also notify the department and the charging party of the ending date of the process. A respondent who fails to provide this notification is barred from raising the defense that the statute of limitations has run unless one year plus a period of time equal to the suspension period has passed.</p><p>(c) The running of the one-year limitation period is suspended during the applicable time period under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.331#stat.363A.331.2">363A.331, subdivision 2</a>, during which a civil action may not be brought.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Basis for filing a claim.</span></h2><p>For purposes of subdivision 3, the first application of an unfair discriminatory practice, employment policy, or seniority system to a new person establishes a basis for the filing of a claim by that person.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.5"><a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.5">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Alternative dispute resolution.</span></h2><p>Charging parties and respondents shall be offered and may request an opportunity to resolve a charge through mediation or another alternative dispute resolution process. Neutrals providing mediation and alternative dispute resolution processes for parties are bound by the Code of Ethics for Court-Annexed ADR Neutrals. The running of the 12-month period during which the commissioner must make a determination of probable cause to credit allegations is suspended during a period of time specified by the commissioner during which the parties are involved in mediation or other alternative dispute resolution process that has been sanctioned by the commissioner.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.6"><a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.6">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Charge processing.</span></h2><p>(a) Consistent with paragraph (h), the commissioner shall promptly inquire into the truth of the allegations of the charge. The commissioner shall make an immediate inquiry when a charge alleges actual or threatened physical violence. The commissioner shall also make an inquiry when it appears that a charge is frivolous or without merit and shall dismiss those charges.</p><p>(b) The commissioner shall give priority to investigating and processing those charges, in the order below, which the commissioner determines have the following characteristics:</p><p>(1) there is evidence of irreparable harm if immediate action is not taken;</p><p>(2) there is evidence that the respondent has intentionally engaged in a reprisal;</p><p>(3) a significant number of recent charges have been filed against the respondent;</p><p>(4) the respondent is a government entity;</p><p>(5) there is potential for broadly promoting the policies of this chapter; or</p><p>(6) the charge is supported by substantial and credible documentation, witnesses, or other evidence.</p><p>The commissioner shall inform charging parties of these priorities and shall tell each party if their charge is a priority case or not.</p><p>On other charges the commissioner shall make a determination within 12 months after the charge was filed as to whether or not there is probable cause to credit the allegation of unfair discriminatory practices.</p><p>(c) If the commissioner determines after investigation that no probable cause exists to credit the allegations of the unfair discriminatory practice, the commissioner shall, within ten days of the determination, serve upon the charging party and respondent written notice of the determination. Within 30 days after receipt of notice, the charging party may request in writing, on forms prepared by the department, that the commissioner reconsider the determination. The request shall contain a brief statement of the reasons for and new evidence in support of the request for reconsideration. At the time of submission of the request to the commissioner, the charging party shall deliver or mail to the respondent a copy of the request for reconsideration. The commissioner shall reaffirm, reverse, or vacate and remand for further consideration the determination of no probable cause within 20 days after receipt of the request for reconsideration, and shall within ten days notify in writing the charging party and respondent of the decision to reaffirm, reverse, or vacate and remand for further consideration.</p><p>A decision by the commissioner that no probable cause exists to credit the allegations of an unfair discriminatory practice shall not be appealed to the court of appeals pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.34">363A.34</a> or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.68">14.68</a>.</p><p>(d) If the commissioner determines after investigation that probable cause exists to credit the allegations of unfair discriminatory practices, the commissioner shall serve on the respondent and the respondent's attorney if the respondent is represented by counsel, by first class mail, or electronically with the receiving party's consent, a notice setting forth a short plain written statement of the alleged facts which support the finding of probable cause and an enumeration of the provisions of law allegedly violated. Within 30 days after receipt of notice, the respondent may request in writing, on forms prepared by the department, that the commissioner reconsider the determination. If the commissioner determines that attempts to eliminate the alleged unfair practices through conciliation pursuant to subdivision 8 have been or would be unsuccessful or unproductive, the commissioner may issue a complaint and serve on the respondent, by registered or certified mail, or electronically with the receiving party's consent, a written notice of hearing together with a copy of the complaint, requiring the respondent to answer the allegations of the complaint at a hearing before an administrative law judge at a time and place specified in the notice, not less than ten days after service of said complaint. A copy of the notice shall be furnished to the charging party and the attorney general.</p><p>(e) If, at any time after the filing of a charge, the commissioner has reason to believe that a respondent has engaged in any unfair discriminatory practice, the commissioner may file a petition in the district court in a county in which the subject of the complaint occurs, or in a county in which a respondent resides or transacts business, seeking appropriate temporary relief against the respondent, pending final determination of proceedings under this chapter, including an order or decree restraining the respondent from doing or procuring an act tending to render ineffectual an order the commissioner may enter with respect to the complaint. The court shall have power to grant temporary relief or a restraining order as it deems just and proper, but no relief or order extending beyond ten days shall be granted except by consent of the respondent or after hearing upon notice to the respondent and a finding by the court that there is reasonable cause to believe that the respondent has engaged in a discriminatory practice. Except as modified by subdivisions 1 to 9 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.06#stat.363A.06.4">363A.06, subdivision 4</a>, the Minnesota Rules of Civil Procedure shall apply to an application, and the district court shall have authority to grant or deny the relief sought on conditions as it deems just and equitable. All hearings under subdivisions 1 to 9 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.06#stat.363A.06.4">363A.06, subdivision 4</a>, shall be given precedence as nearly as practicable over all other pending civil actions.</p><p>(f) If a lessor, after engaging in a discriminatory practice defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.09#stat.363A.09.1">363A.09, subdivision 1, clause (1)</a>, leases or rents a dwelling unit to a person who has no knowledge of the practice or of the existence of a charge with respect to the practice, the lessor shall be liable for actual damages sustained by a person by reason of a final order as provided in subdivisions 1 to 9 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.06#stat.363A.06.4">363A.06, subdivision 4</a>, requiring the person to be evicted from the dwelling unit.</p><p>(g) In any complaint issued under subdivisions 1 to 9 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.06#stat.363A.06.4">363A.06, subdivision 4</a>, the commissioner may seek relief for a class of individuals affected by an unfair discriminatory practice occurring on or after a date one year prior to the filing of the charge from which the complaint originates.</p><p>(h) The commissioner may adopt policies to determine which charges are processed and the order in which charges are processed based on their particular social or legal significance, administrative convenience, difficulty of resolution, or other standard consistent with the provisions of this chapter.</p><p>(i) The chief administrative law judge shall adopt policies to provide sanctions for intentional and frivolous delay caused by any charging party or respondent in an investigation, hearing, or any other aspect of proceedings before the department under this chapter.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.7"><a title="Link to Subd. 7." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.7">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Application of rules.</span></h2><p>Rules adopted pursuant to this subdivision apply to cases pending before the commissioner on the date of adoption.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.8"><a title="Link to Subd. 8." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.8">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Attempts to eliminate unfair practices.</span></h2><p>The commissioner, in complying with subdivision 6, shall endeavor to eliminate the unfair discriminatory practice through education, conference, conciliation and persuasion at the place where the practice occurred, or the respondent resides or has a principal place of business.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.9"><a title="Link to Subd. 9." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.9">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Access to documents.</span></h2><p>The commissioner shall provide the respondent with a copy of the charge. The charging party or the party's representative may review the answer of the respondent to the charge submitted pursuant to subdivision 1. The department shall make these documents available to the charging party.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.28.10"><a title="Link to Subd. 10." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.28.10">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Disparate impact cases in employment.</span></h2><p>If the complaining party has met its burden of showing that an employment practice is responsible for a statistically significant adverse impact on a particular class of persons protected by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08#stat.363A.08.2">363A.08, subdivision 2</a>, an employer must justify that practice by demonstrating that the practice is manifestly related to the job or significantly furthers an important business purpose. Upon establishment of this justification, the charging party may prevail upon demonstration of the existence of a comparably effective practice that the court finds would cause a significantly lesser adverse impact on the identified protected class.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.28.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.5.0">1955 c 516 s 5</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.5.0">1961 c 428 s 5</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/517/#laws.0.8.0">1982 c 517 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.59.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/247/#laws.0.143.0">1983 c 247 s 143</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.326.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 326</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 23 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 129 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/141/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 141 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/245/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 245 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/375/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 375 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 660 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.1.37.0">1989 c 209 art 1 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 280 s 9</a>-14,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.8.11.0">1989 c 329 art 8 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 567 s 3</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 527 s 12</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 22 s 8</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/277/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 277 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/630/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 630 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 212 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.2.60.0">1996 c 305 art 2 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/182/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 182 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 194 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/159/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 159 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/80/#laws.0.1.0">2017 c 80 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.3.14.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 3 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/105/#laws.0.13.0">2024 c 105 s 13</a></p></div><p><b>NOTE: </b>Causes of action under subdivision 1 which seek to remedy activity proscribed under ERISA were found preempted by the federal Employee Retirement Income Security Act (ERISA) in <i>Alwin v. Sprint Communications Co.</i>, 870 F.Supp. 275 (D. Minn. 1994).</p><p><b>NOTE: </b>Causes of action under subdivision 1 which seek to remedy violations of the duty of fair representation were found preempted by the federal National Labor Relations Act in <i>Patterson v. IATSE Local 13</i>, 754 F.Supp.2d 1043 (D.
