Minn. 2010).</p><div class="section" id="stat.363A.29"><h1 class="shn">363A.29 HEARINGS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Conduct of hearings.</span></h2><p>A determination issued by the commissioner may be heard as a contested case, except that the report of the administrative law judge shall be binding on all parties to the proceeding and if appropriate shall be implemented by an order as provided for in subdivision 3. The party contesting the determination issued by the commissioner may file a request with the commissioner to appear at a hearing on the party's own behalf or through a private attorney. The commissioner shall forward the request for hearing to the Office of Administrative Hearings, which shall promptly set the matter for hearing. The hearing shall be conducted at a place designated by the commissioner, within the county where the unfair discriminatory practice occurred or where the respondent resides or has a principal place of business. The hearing shall be conducted in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>, and is subject to appeal in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.68">14.68</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Tolling during investigation.</span></h2><p>The commissioner shall make a determination of probable cause or no probable cause within one year of the filing of a case in which the time has not been tolled. The amount of time during which a case is involved in significant settlement negotiations, is being investigated by another enforcement agency under a work sharing agreement, or has been referred to mediation or to a local human rights commission for no fault grievance processing is tolled. Tolling of the time during settlement negotiations requires written approval of the charging party or the party's attorney.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Determination of discriminatory practice.</span></h2><p>The administrative law judge shall make findings of fact and conclusions of law, and if the administrative law judge finds that the respondent has engaged in an unfair discriminatory practice, the administrative law judge shall issue an order directing the respondent to cease and desist from the unfair discriminatory practice found to exist and to take such affirmative action as in the judgment of the administrative law judge will effectuate the purposes of this chapter. The order shall be a final decision of the department.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Civil penalty; punitive damages.</span></h2><p>(a) The administrative law judge shall order any respondent found to be in violation of any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.19">363A.19</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.28#stat.363A.28.10">363A.28, subdivision 10</a>, to pay a civil penalty to the state. This penalty is in addition to compensatory and punitive damages to be paid to an aggrieved party. The administrative law judge shall determine the amount of the civil penalty to be paid, taking into account the seriousness and extent of the violation, the public harm occasioned by the violation, whether the violation was intentional, and the financial resources of the respondent. Any penalties imposed under this provision shall be paid into the general fund of the state. In all cases where the administrative law judge finds that the respondent has engaged in an unfair discriminatory practice, the administrative law judge shall order the respondent to pay an aggrieved party, who has suffered discrimination, compensatory damages in an amount up to three times the actual damages sustained. In all cases, the administrative law judge may also order the respondent to pay an aggrieved party, who has suffered discrimination, damages for mental anguish or suffering and reasonable attorney's fees, in addition to punitive damages in an amount not more than $25,000 pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/549.20">549.20</a>.</p><p>(b) In any case where a political subdivision is a respondent, the total of punitive damages awarded an aggrieved party may not exceed $25,000 and in that case if there are two or more respondents the punitive damages may be apportioned among them. Punitive damages may only be assessed against a political subdivision in its capacity as a corporate entity and no regular or ex officio member of a governing body of a political subdivision shall be personally liable for payment of punitive damages pursuant to subdivisions 3 to 6.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.5"><a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.5">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Other remedies.</span></h2><p>In addition to the remedies in subdivision 4, in a case involving discrimination in:</p><p>(1) employment, the administrative law judge may order the hiring, reinstatement or upgrading of an aggrieved party, who has suffered discrimination, with or without back pay, admission or restoration to membership in a labor organization, or admission to or participation in an apprenticeship training program, on-the-job training program, or other retraining program, or any other relief the administrative law judge deems just and equitable; or</p><p>(2) housing, the administrative law judge may order the sale, lease, or rental of the housing accommodation or other real property to an aggrieved party, who has suffered discrimination, or the sale, lease, or rental of a like accommodation or other real property owned by or under the control of the person against whom the complaint was filed, according to terms as listed with a real estate broker, or if no such listing has been made, as otherwise advertised or offered by the vendor or lessor, or any other relief the administrative law judge deems just and equitable.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.6"><a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.6">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Service of findings.</span></h2><p>The administrative law judge shall cause the findings of fact, conclusions of law, and order to be served on the respondent personally, on the charging party by registered or certified mail, and shall furnish copies to the attorney general and the commissioner.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.7"><a title="Link to Subd. 7." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.7">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Dismissal of hearing.</span></h2><p>If the administrative law judge makes findings of fact, conclusions of law, and an order in favor of the respondent, the order shall be a final decision of the department.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.8"><a title="Link to Subd. 8." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.8">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Respondents subject to state licensing or regulatory power.</span></h2><p>In the case of a respondent which is subject to the licensing or regulatory power of the state or any political subdivision or agency thereof, if the administrative law judge determines that the respondent has engaged in a discriminatory practice, and if the respondent does not cease to engage in such discriminatory practice, the commissioner may so certify to the licensing or regulatory agency. Unless such determination of discriminatory practice is reversed in the course of judicial review, a final determination is binding on the licensing or regulatory agency. Such agency may take appropriate administrative action, including suspension or revocation of the respondent's license or certificate of public convenience and necessity, if the agency is otherwise authorized to take such action.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.9"><a title="Link to Subd. 9." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.9">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Public contracts.</span></h2><p>In the case of a respondent which is a party to a public contract, if the administrative law judge determines that the respondent has engaged in a discriminatory practice, the commissioner may so certify to the contract letting agency. Unless the finding of a discriminatory practice is reversed in the course of judicial review, a final determination is binding on the contract letting agency and the agency may take appropriate administrative action, including the imposition of financial penalties or termination of the contract, in whole or in part, if the agency is otherwise authorized to take the action.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.10"><a title="Link to Subd. 10." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.10">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Subpoenas.</span></h2><p>After the issuance of a complaint pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.28#stat.363A.28.6">363A.28, subdivision 6</a>, a charging party or a respondent may request that the administrative law judge issue subpoenas requiring the presence of witnesses or the production for examination of books or papers not privileged and relevant to any matter in question at the hearing.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.29.11"><a title="Link to Subd. 11." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.29.11">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Litigation and hearing costs.</span></h2><p>The administrative law judge shall order a respondent who is determined to have engaged in an unfair discriminatory practice to reimburse the department and the attorney general for all appropriate litigation and hearing costs expended in preparing for and conducting the hearing, unless payment of the costs would impose a financial hardship on the respondent. Appropriate costs include but are not limited to the costs of services rendered by the attorney general, private attorneys if engaged by the department, administrative law judges, court reporters, and expert witnesses as well as the costs of transcripts and other necessary supplies and materials.</p><p>Money reimbursed to the Department of Human Rights under this subdivision must be paid into the state treasury and credited to a special revenue account. Money in that account is appropriated to the commissioner of human rights to the extent the reimbursements were made to cover the department's costs and are available for the department's activities in enforcing the Minnesota Human Rights Act.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.29.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.20.0">1967 c 897 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/975/#laws.0.11.0">1969 c 975 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.9.0">1973 c 729 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/301/#laws.0.3.0">1976 c 301 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/540/#laws.0.4.0">1980 c 540 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/364/#laws.0.2.0">1981 c 364 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.201.0">1983 c 301 s 201</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/567/#laws.0.4.0">1984 c 567 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.56.0">1985 c 248 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/375/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 375 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.7.0">1988 c 660 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.9.34.0">1992 c 513 art 9 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/390/#laws.0.34.0">1996 c 390 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/227/#laws.0.22.0">1999 c 227 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/215/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 215 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/105/#laws.0.14.0">2024 c 105 s 14</a>,15</p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.30"><h1 class="shn">363A.30 DISTRICT COURT, REVIEW ORDERS OF PANEL OR EXAMINER; ENFORCEMENT; MISDEMEANOR.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.30.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.30.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appeal.</span></h2><p>The commissioner or a person aggrieved by a final decision of the department reached after a hearing held pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.29">363A.29</a> may seek judicial review in accordance with chapter 14. The attorney general shall represent on appeal, a charging party who prevailed at a hearing authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.29#stat.363A.29.2">363A.29, subdivision 2</a>, if the charging party requests representation within ten days after receipt of the petition for appeal.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.30.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.30.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Review procedure.</span></h2><p>The judicial review proceedings shall be in accordance with chapter 14.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.30.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.30.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2><p>When a respondent fails or refuses to comply with a final decision of the department, the commissioner may file with the court administrator of district court in the judicial district in which the hearing was held a petition requesting the court to order the respondent to comply with the order of the department. Thereupon the court shall issue an order to show cause directed to the respondent why an order directing compliance should not be issued. If the panel or examiner has ordered an award of damages pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.30">363A.30</a>, the court shall enter judgment on the order or modified order in the same manner as in the case of an order of the district court, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/546.27">546.27</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.30.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.30.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Unfair discriminatory practice a misdemeanor.</span></h2><p>In addition to all other remedies provided under this chapter, every person who commits an unfair discriminatory act as set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.11">363A.11</a>, or aids, abets, incites, compels, or coerces another to do so, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.30.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.21.0">1967 c 897 s 21</a>,22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/975/#laws.0.14.0">1969 c 975 s 14</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.10.0">1973 c 729 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/408/#laws.0.5.0">1977 c 408 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/247/#laws.0.144.0">1983 c 247 s 144</a>,145; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/567/#laws.0.6.0">1984 c 567 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.10.0">1988 c 660 s 10</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.31"><h1 class="shn">363A.31 LIMITATIONS ON WAIVER.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.31.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.31.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prospective waiver prohibited.</span></h2><p>Any provision, whether oral or written, of a lease, contract, or other agreement or instrument which purports to be a waiver by an individual of any right or remedy provided in this chapter is contrary to public policy and void if the waiver or release purports to waive claims arising out of acts or practices which occur after the execution of the waiver or release.</p><p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.31.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.31.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rescission of waiver.</span></h2><p>A waiver or release of rights or remedies secured by this chapter which purports to apply to claims arising out of acts or practices prior to, or concurrent with, the execution of the waiver or release may be rescinded within 15 calendar days of its execution, except that a waiver or release given in settlement of a claim filed with the department or with another administrative agency or judicial body is valid and final upon execution. A waiving or releasing party shall be informed in writing of the right to rescind the waiver or release. To be effective, the rescission must be in writing and delivered to the waived or released party by hand, electronically with the receiving party's consent, or by mail within the 15-day period. If delivered by mail, the rescission must be:</p><p>(1) postmarked within the 15-day period;</p><p>(2) properly addressed to the waived or released party; and</p><p>(3) sent by certified mail return receipt requested.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.31.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/567/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 567 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/175/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 175 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.3.16.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 3 s 16</a></p></div><p><b>NOTE:</b> Subdivision 1 (formerly section 363.031) was found preempted by federal law with regard to arbitration agreements in <i>Johnson v. Piper Jaffray, Inc.</i>, 530 N.W.2d 790 (Minn. 1995).</p><div class="section" id="stat.363A.32"><h1 class="shn">363A.32 DEPARTMENT ATTORNEY.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.32.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.32.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Attorney general; privileged communication.</span></h2><p>The attorney general shall be the attorney for the department. When a matter has been referred to the attorney general by the commissioner after a finding of probable cause or for the purpose of interim relief, communications between members of the attorney general's office and charging parties or members of a class formed pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.28#stat.363A.28.6">363A.28, subdivision 6, paragraph (g)</a>, are privileged as would be a communication between an attorney and a client.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.32.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.32.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Legal representation costs.</span></h2><p>The Department of Human Rights may not be charged by the attorney general for legal representation on behalf of complaining parties who have filed a charge of discrimination with the department. This subdivision is effective retroactive to July 1, 1989. The department does not have an obligation to pay for any services rendered by the attorney general since July 1, 1985, in excess of the amounts already paid for those services.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.32.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.27.0">1967 c 897 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.11.0">1988 c 660 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.90.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 90</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.33"><h1 class="shn">363A.33 COURT ACTIONS, SUITS BY PRIVATE PARTIES, INTERVENTION, DISTRICT COURT JURISDICTION, ATTORNEY'S FEES, AND COSTS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Court actions, suits by private parties, intervention.</span></h2><p>The commissioner or a person may bring a civil action seeking redress for an unfair discriminatory practice directly to district court. In addition, a person may bring a civil action:</p><p>(1) within 90 days after receipt of notice that the commissioner has dismissed a charge because it is frivolous or without merit, because the charging party has failed to provide required information, because the commissioner has determined that further use of department resources is not warranted, or because the commissioner has determined that there is no probable cause to credit the allegations contained in a charge filed with the commissioner;</p><p>(2) within 90 days after receipt of notice that the commissioner has reaffirmed a determination of no probable cause if the charging party requested a reconsideration of the no probable cause determination, or has decided not to reopen a dismissed case that the charging party has asked to be reopened; or</p><p>(3) after 45 days from the filing of a charge pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.28#stat.363A.28.1">363A.28, subdivision 1</a>, if a hearing has not been held pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.29">363A.29</a> or if the commissioner has not entered into a conciliation agreement to which the charging party is a signator. The charging party shall notify the commissioner of an intention to bring a civil action, which shall be commenced within 90 days of giving the notice.</p><p>For purposes of clauses (1) and (2), receipt of notice is presumed to be five days from the date of service by mail of the written notice.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Treatment of separate issues in same charge.</span></h2><p>If the commissioner has issued both probable cause and no probable cause determinations on separate issues in the same charge, the charging party may, if a hearing is held, require that all matters be heard at the hearing or may bring a civil action for the no probable cause charges at the same time as the probable cause charges under the rules and time frames that govern the probable cause charges.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Summons and complaints in a civil action.</span></h2><p>A charging party bringing a civil action shall mail by registered or certified mail, or electronically with the receiving party's consent, a copy of the summons and complaint to the commissioner, and upon their receipt the commissioner shall terminate all proceedings in the department relating to the charge. No charge shall be filed or reinstituted with the commissioner after a civil action relating to the same unfair discriminatory practice has been brought unless the civil action has been dismissed without prejudice.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Court-appointed attorney for complaining party and costs.</span></h2><p>Upon application by the complaining party to the district court at a special term and under circumstances the court deems just, the court may appoint an attorney for the person and may authorize the commencement of the action without payment of fees, costs, or security.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.5"><a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.5">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Department as intervenor in civil action.</span></h2><p>Upon timely application, the court may permit the department to intervene in a civil action brought pursuant to this section upon certification that the case is of general public importance.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.6"><a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.6">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">District court jurisdiction.</span></h2><p>Any action brought pursuant to this section shall be filed in the district court of the county wherein the unlawful discriminatory practice is alleged to have been committed or where the respondent resides or has a principal place of business.</p><p>A person bringing a civil action seeking redress for an unfair discriminatory practice or a respondent is entitled to a jury trial.</p><p>If the court or jury finds that the respondent has engaged in an unfair discriminatory practice, it shall issue an order or verdict directing appropriate relief as provided by this section.</p><p>When the court issues an order providing for payment to the state of a civil penalty pursuant to this section, it shall serve a copy of that order upon the attorney general at the same time as it makes service upon the parties.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.7"><a title="Link to Subd. 7." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.7">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Attorney's fees and costs.</span></h2><p>In any action or proceeding brought pursuant to this section the court, in its discretion, may allow the prevailing party a reasonable attorney's fee as part of the costs. In any case brought by the department, the court shall order a respondent who is determined to have engaged in an unfair discriminatory practice to reimburse the department and the attorney general for all appropriate litigation and court costs expended in preparing for and conducting the hearing, unless payment of the costs would impose a financial hardship on the respondent. Appropriate costs include but are not limited to the costs of services rendered by the attorney general, private attorneys if engaged by the department, court costs, court reporters, and expert witnesses as well as the costs of transcripts and other necessary supplies and materials.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.8"><a title="Link to Subd. 8." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.8">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Civil penalty; punitive damages.</span></h2><p>(a) The court shall order any respondent found to be in violation of any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.19">363A.19</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.28">363A.28</a>, subdivision 10, to pay a civil penalty to the state. This penalty is in addition to all damages recoverable at law and punitive damages to be paid to an aggrieved party. The court shall determine the amount of the civil penalty to be paid, taking into account the seriousness and extent of the violation, the public harm occasioned by the violation, whether the violation was intentional, and the financial resources of the respondent. Any penalties imposed under this provision shall be paid into the general fund of the state. In all cases where the court finds that the respondent has engaged in an unfair discriminatory practice, the court shall order the respondent to pay an aggrieved party who has suffered discrimination compensatory damages, including mental anguish or suffering, in an amount up to three times the actual damages sustained. In all cases, the court may also order the respondent to pay an aggrieved party punitive damages pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/549.20">549.20</a>. In any case under this chapter that is decided by a jury, the jury shall determine the amount of all damages to be awarded.</p><p>(b) In any case where a political subdivision is a respondent, the total of punitive damages awarded an aggrieved party may not exceed $25,000 and if there are two or more respondents the punitive damages may be apportioned among them. Punitive damages may only be assessed against a political subdivision in its capacity as a corporate entity and no regular or ex officio member of a governing body of a political subdivision shall be personally liable for payment of punitive damages pursuant to this section.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.33.9"><a title="Link to Subd. 9." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.33.9">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Other remedies.</span></h2><p>In addition to the remedies in subdivision 8, where a case involves discrimination in:</p><p>(1) employment, the court may order: (i) the hiring, reinstatement, or upgrading of an aggrieved party who has suffered discrimination, with or without back pay; (ii) admission or restoration to membership in a labor organization; (iii) admission to or participation in an apprenticeship training program, on-the-job training program, or other retraining program; or (iv) any other relief the court deems just and equitable; or</p><p>(2) housing, the court may order: (i) the sale, lease, or rental of the housing accommodation or other real property to an aggrieved party who has suffered discrimination; (ii) the sale, lease, or rental of a like accommodation or other real property owned by or under the control of the person against whom the complaint was filed, according to the terms as listed with a real estate broker, or if no such listing has been made, as advertised or offered by the vendor or lessor; or (iii) any other relief the court deems just and equitable.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.33.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.18.0">1973 c 729 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/301/#laws.0.4.0">1976 c 301 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/455/#laws.0.85.0">1977 c 455 s 85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/793/#laws.0.74.0">1978 c 793 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/330/#laws.0.6.0">1981 c 330 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/567/#laws.0.8.0">1984 c 567 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.12.0">1988 c 660 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.19.0">1989 c 280 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.9.35.0">1992 c 513 art 9 s 35</a>,36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/233/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 233 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.3.17.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 3 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/105/#laws.0.16.0">2024 c 105 s 16</a>-19</p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.331"><h1 class="shn">363A.331 ACTIONS INVOLVING ARCHITECTURAL BARRIERS THAT LIMIT ACCESSIBILITY.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.331.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.331.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p><p>(b) "Accessibility requirements under law" means requirements governing removal of architectural barriers that limit access to business establishments or public accommodations by persons with disabilities under the following laws or rules:</p><p>(1) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.11#stat.363A.11.3">363A.11, subdivision 3</a>, clause (4) or (5);</p><p>(2) United States Code, title 42, section 12182, paragraph (b), clause (2), subparagraph (A), subclause (iv) or (v); or</p><p>(3) Code of Federal Regulations, title 28, section 36.304 or 36.305.</p><p>(c) "Certified professional" means a licensed, registered, or otherwise certified professional with demonstrated knowledge of accessibility requirements under law.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.331.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.331.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice of architectural barrier.</span></h2><p>(a) Before bringing a civil action under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.33">363A.33</a>, a person who is an attorney or is represented by an attorney and who alleges that a business establishment or place of public accommodation has violated accessibility requirements under law must provide a notice of architectural barrier consistent with subdivision 3. The notice of architectural barrier must be dated and must:</p><p>(1) cite the law alleged to be violated;</p><p>(2) identify each architectural barrier that is the subject of an alleged violation and specify its location on the premises;</p><p>(3) provide a reasonable time for a response, which may not be less than 60 days; and</p><p>(4) comply with subdivision 3.</p><p>(b) A notice described in paragraph (a) must not include a request or demand for money or an offer or agreement to accept money, but may offer to engage in settlement negotiations before litigation.</p><p>(c) A civil action may not be brought before expiration of the period to respond provided in the notice under paragraph (a), clause (3). Subject to paragraph (d), a civil action may be brought after the response time provided in the notice.</p><p>(d) If, within the response time provided under paragraph (a), clause (3), the business establishment or place of public accommodation indicates in writing an intent to remove the barrier but can demonstrate that weather prevents a timely removal, a civil action may not be brought before 30 days after the date of the response time in the notice, provided the business establishment or place of public accommodation specifies in writing the steps that will be taken to remove the barrier and the date by which the barrier will be removed.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.331.2a"><a title="Link to Subd. 2a." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.331.2a">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Representation by attorney after action brought.</span></h2><p>If a person who is not represented by an attorney retains an attorney within 60 days after a civil action under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.33">363A.33</a> is brought, the attorney must provide a dated notice to the defendant that includes the items required under subdivision 2, paragraph (a), clauses (1) and (2), and the accessibility audit portion of the form under subdivision 3. The notice must include additional time for the defendant to serve an answer to the complaint or amend a previous answer to the complaint, which must be at least 60 days after the date of the notice or service of the complaint, whichever is later. If the civil action has not been filed with the court, the action must not be filed until expiration of the time provided for in the notice. The time period for serving an answer to the complaint under <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/cp/id/12/">rule 12</a> of the Rules of Civil Procedure and proceedings under the complaint are stayed for the period of time specified in the notice.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.331.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.331.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Statutory short form; notice of architectural barrier.</span></h2><p>A notice of an architectural barrier must be in the following, or a substantially similar, form:</p><p>"This notice is to advise you of a claim that [insert name of business establishment or place of public accommodation] is in violation of the Americans with Disabilities Act and/or the Minnesota Human Rights Act for failure to remove one or more architectural barriers limiting access to the premises by persons with disabilities.</p><p><b>Allegation of Violation</b></p><p>The undersigned attorney represents [insert client's name or organization], who alleges that [insert name of business establishment or place of public accommodation] is in violation of [insert citation of all statute(s) and rule(s) alleged to be violated] because [insert client's name] attempted on [insert date(s)] to access [insert name of business establishment or place of public accommodation] but was unable to or was deterred from doing so due to the following architectural barrier or barriers limiting access by persons with disabilities: [insert description of each architectural barrier that is the subject of the alleged violation and its location on the premises].</p><p><b>Accessibility Audit</b></p><p>Licensed, registered, or otherwise certified professionals with knowledge of the Americans with Disabilities Act and Minnesota Human Rights Act requirements regarding physical barriers to access may be available to conduct an audit of your business establishment or place of public accommodation to advise you regarding compliance with the law. For more information, contact the Minnesota State Council on Disability at 1-800-945-8913 or visit MSCOD's website at http://www.disability.state.mn.us.</p><p><b>Response</b></p><p>Please contact, or have your attorney contact, the undersigned attorney no later than [insert date by which response is required] to provide a response or if you would like to engage in prelitigation settlement negotiations. Failure to respond to this notice may result in a lawsuit being filed against you."</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.331.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.331.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Affirmative defense; challenging audit.</span></h2><p>(a) In a civil action brought against a business establishment or place of public accommodation for violation of accessibility requirements under law, the defendant has an affirmative defense to the action if the defendant demonstrates that:</p><p>(1) the architectural barrier has been removed in a manner that complies with accessibility requirements under law;</p><p>(2) compliance with the accessibility requirements under law is not readily achievable or cannot be accomplished by alternative means; or</p><p>(3) the alleged architectural barrier does not violate accessibility requirements under law.</p><p>(b) In a civil action brought against a business establishment or place of public accommodation for violation of accessibility requirements under law, a plaintiff challenging a finding in an audit, performed by a certified professional, that an alleged architectural barrier complies with accessibility requirements under law, or that compliance is not readily achievable or alternative means cannot be employed, has the burden to show that the architectural barrier does not comply with those accessibility requirements or that compliance is readily achievable or can be accomplished by alternative means.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.331.5"><a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.331.5">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2><p>(a) Subdivisions 2 and 3 do not apply to:</p><p>(1) a person who is not an attorney and is not represented by an attorney;</p><p>(2) attorneys representing the state or a political subdivision of the state; or</p><p>(3) a person who is challenging a finding contained in an audit prepared by a certified professional.</p><p>(b) This section does not affect the right of a person to file a charge with the commissioner or the right of a person who has filed a charge to bring a civil action authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.33#stat.363A.33.1">363A.33, subdivision 1</a>.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.331.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/159/#laws.0.2.0">2016 c 159 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/80/#laws.0.2.0">2017 c 80 s 2</a>-4</p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.34"><h1 class="shn">363A.34 NOTICE OF APPEAL TO COMMISSIONER.</h1><p>In any case that is appealed to the supreme court or the court of appeals in which an issue is raised under this chapter, the party raising the issue shall serve a copy of the notice of appeal on the commissioner. At the time of filing a notice of appeal or other papers, documents, or briefs in the case, a party shall file proof of service of the papers, documents, or briefs upon the commissioner.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.34.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.14.0">1988 c 660 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.20.0">1989 c 280 s 20</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.35"><h1 class="shn">363A.35 ACCESS TO CASE FILES.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.35.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.35.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General provisions.</span></h2><p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.39">13.39</a>, and except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.06#stat.363A.06.4">363A.06, subdivision 4</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.28#stat.363A.28.9">363A.28, subdivision 9</a>, the availability of human rights investigative data to persons other than department employees is governed by this section.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.35.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.35.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Access to open files.</span></h2><p>(a) Except as otherwise provided in this subdivision, human rights investigative data contained in an open case file are confidential data on individuals or protected nonpublic data. The name and address of the charging party and respondent, factual basis of the allegations, and the statute under which the action is brought are private data on individuals or nonpublic data but are accessible to the charging party and the respondent.</p><p>(b) After a charge has been filed, the commissioner may disclose information to persons as the commissioner deems necessary (1) to facilitate investigation or disposition of the charge, or (2) to promote public health or safety. The commissioner may also disclose data about an open case file to another governmental entity to assist that entity or the department in processing a complaint or to eliminate duplication of efforts in the investigation of the same or similar facts as alleged in the charge. To the extent that data are disclosed to other governmental entities, it must be stipulated that section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.03#stat.13.03.4">13.03, subdivision 4</a>, applies to the classification of the data.</p><p>(c) After making a finding of probable cause, the commissioner may make human rights investigative data contained in an open case file accessible to a person, government agency, or the public if access will aid the investigative and enforcement process.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.35.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.35.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Access to closed files.</span></h2><p>(a) Except as otherwise provided in this subdivision, human rights investigative data contained in a closed case file are private data on individuals or nonpublic data. The name and address of the charging party and respondent, factual basis of the allegations, the statute under which the action is brought, the part of the summary of the investigation that does not contain identifying data on a person other than the complainant or respondent, and the commissioner's memorandum determining whether probable cause has been shown are public data.</p><p>(b) The commissioner may make human rights investigative data contained in a closed case file inaccessible to the charging party or the respondent in order to protect medical or other security interests of the parties or third persons.</p><p>(c) Except for paragraph (b), when the charging party files a case in district court, the commissioner may provide private data or nonpublic data in a closed case file to the charging party and respondent.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.35.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.35.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Charging party access.</span></h2><p>Data comprised of materials and documentation provided by a charging party that is part of an open or closed case file is accessible to the charging party in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.04#stat.13.04.3">13.04, subdivision 3</a>. The charging party may consent to the release of the data to the charging party's attorney or other legal representative.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.35.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.327.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 327</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/670/#laws.0.13.0">1988 c 670 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/259/#laws.1.52.0">1995 c 259 art 1 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/172/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 172 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/194/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 194 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/5/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2019 c 5 art 2 s 9</a></p></div><h1 class="fgh">COMPLIANCE REQUIREMENTS</h1><div class="section" id="stat.363A.36"><h1 class="shn">363A.36 CERTIFICATES OF COMPLIANCE FOR PUBLIC CONTRACTS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.36.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.36.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope of application.</span></h2><p class="in">(a) No department, agency of the state, the Metropolitan Council, or agency subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.143#stat.473.143.1">473.143, subdivision 1</a>, shall execute a contract for goods or services in excess of $100,000 with a business that has 40 or more full-time employees in this state or a state where the business has its primary place of business on a single day during the prior 12 months, unless the business has a workforce certificate from the commissioner of human rights or has certified in writing that it is exempt. Determinations of exempt status shall be made by the commissioner of human rights. A certificate is valid for four years. A municipality as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.01#stat.466.01.1">466.01, subdivision 1</a>, that receives state money for any reason is encouraged to prepare and implement an affirmative action plan for the employment of people with disabilities, people of color, and women, and to submit the plan to the commissioner.</p><p class="in">(b) This section does not apply to contracts entered into by the State Board of Investment for investment options under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.645">356.645</a>.</p><p>(c) The commissioner shall issue a certificate of compliance or notice of denial within 15 days of the application submitted by the business or firm.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.36.1a"><a title="Link to Subd. 1a." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.36.1a">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Scope of application; state capital funding.</span></h2><p>(a) An agency that uses state money to pay for part or all of a capital project is subject to and must comply with the restrictions in subdivision 1, for contracts exceeding $100,000. A political subdivision that uses state money to pay for part or all of a capital project is subject to and must comply with the restrictions in subdivision 1, for contracts exceeding $250,000.</p><p>(b) For the purposes of this subdivision, the following terms have the meanings given them:</p><p>(1) "agency" means a state board, commission, authority, department, or other agency of the executive branch of state government; the Metropolitan Council; the Minnesota Historical Society; the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities; or the University of Minnesota;</p><p>(2) "capital project" means the acquisition and betterment of land and buildings and other public improvements in the state, including acquisition of real property or an interest in real property, predesign, design, engineering, site preparation and related environmental work, renovation, construction, furnishing, and equipping;</p><p>(3) "political subdivision" means a county, home rule charter or statutory city, town, school district, metropolitan or regional agency other than the Metropolitan Council, public corporation established in law, or other special or limited purpose district created or authorized by law; and</p><p>(4) "state money" means the proceeds of state general obligation bonds issued under article XI, section 5, clause (a), of the Minnesota Constitution.</p><p>(c) This subdivision applies to a capital project or discrete phase of a capital project for which state money has been appropriated on or after January 1, 2022.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.36.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.36.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Filing fee; account; appropriation.</span></h2><p>The commissioner shall collect a $250 fee for each certificate of compliance issued by the commissioner or the commissioner's designated agent. The proceeds of the fee must be deposited in a human rights fee special revenue account. Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner to fund the cost of issuing certificates and investigating grievances.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.36.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.36.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Violations; remedies.</span></h2><p>Certificates of compliance may be suspended or revoked by the commissioner if a holder of a certificate has not made a good faith effort to implement an affirmative action plan that has been approved by the commissioner. If a contractor does not effectively implement an affirmative action plan approved by the commissioner pursuant to subdivision 1, or fails to make a good faith effort to do so, the commissioner may refuse to approve subsequent plans submitted by that firm or business. If a certificate holder is in violation of this section, the commissioner may impose one or both of the following actions:</p><p>(1) issue fines up to $5,000 per calendar year for each contract; or</p><p>(2) suspend or revoke a certificate of compliance until the contractor has paid all outstanding fines and otherwise complies with this section.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.36.4"><a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.36.4">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Revocation of contract.</span></h2><p>A contract awarded by a department or agency of the state, the Metropolitan Council, or an agency subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.143#stat.473.143.1">473.143, subdivision 1</a>, may be terminated or abridged by the awarding entity because of suspension or revocation of a certificate based upon a contractor's failure to implement or make a good faith effort to implement an affirmative action plan approved by the commissioner under this section. If a contract is awarded to a person who does not have a contract compliance certificate required under subdivision 1, the commissioner may void the contract on behalf of the state.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.36.5"><a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.36.5">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance.</span></h2><p>In the case of a contractor whose certificate of compliance has been suspended, the commissioner shall provide technical assistance that may enable the contractor to be recertified within 90 days after the contractor's certificate has been suspended.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.36.6"><a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.36.6">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Access to data.</span></h2><p>Data submitted to the commissioner related to a certificate of compliance are private data on individuals or nonpublic data with respect to persons other than department employees. The commissioner's decision to issue, not issue, revoke, or suspend or otherwise penalize a certificate holder of a certificate of compliance is public data. Applications, forms, or similar documents submitted by a business seeking a certificate of compliance are public data. The commissioner may disclose data classified as private or nonpublic under this subdivision to other state agencies, statewide systems, and political subdivisions for the purposes of achieving compliance with this section.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.36.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/975/#laws.0.19.0">1969 c 975 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/527/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 527 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/326/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 326 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.377.0">1981 c 356 s 377</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/4/#laws.3.14.0">1Sp1981 c 4 art 3 s 14</a>; art 4 s 33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.8.0">1988 c 660 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.16.0">1989 c 280 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.9.27.0">1989 c 329 art 9 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.244.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 244</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/19/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 19 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.47.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.3.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.1.33.0">1998 c 386 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.3.0">2001 c 186 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/2/#laws.2.5.0">1Sp2003 c 2 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/349/#laws.11.9.0">2008 c 349 art 11 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/72/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 72 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/86/#laws.3.7.0">2013 c 86 art 3 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/296/#laws.13.24.0">2014 c 296 art 13 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.18.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.3.18.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 3 s 18</a>-22</p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.37"><h1 class="shn">363A.37 CERTIFICATES OF COMPLIANCE.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.37.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.37.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2><p>The commissioner shall adopt rules to implement section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.36">363A.36</a> specifying the criteria used to review affirmative action plans and the standards used to review implementation of affirmative action plans. A firm or business certified to be in compliance with affirmative action requirements of a local human rights agency or the federal government shall be deemed to be in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.36">363A.36</a> upon receipt by the commissioner of an affirmative action plan approved by a local human rights agency or the federal government and amendments to the plan which are necessary to address the employment of disabled persons protected by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.37.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.37.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Issuing certificates of compliance.</span></h2><p>The commissioner shall issue a certificate of compliance or a letter stating notice of deficiencies within 15 days after the department has received the information required in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/5000.3560">5000.3560</a>, subpart 1. The notification must state specifically the ways in which the submission fails to meet the requirements of Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/5000.3560">5000.3560</a>, subpart 1. Certificates of compliance shall be issued 15 days after the department has received a revised submission that complies with Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/5000.3560">5000.3560</a>, subpart 1.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.37.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/326/#laws.0.2.0">1981 c 326 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/186/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 186 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/72/#laws.0.2.0">2013 c 72 s 2</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.38"><h1 class="shn">363A.38 MANUFACTURED HOME PARKS.</h1><p>A park owner must comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/327C.02#stat.327C.02.2">327C.02, subdivision 2</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/327C.05">327C.05</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/327C.07#stat.327C.07.4">327C.07, subdivision 4</a>, when adopting or amending a rule concerning the permitted familial status of residents or of buyers of homes offered for in park sale.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.38.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/516/#laws.0.4.0">1955 c 516 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 428 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/584/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 584 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/897/#laws.0.10.0">1967 c 897 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/729/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 729 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/206/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 206 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/351/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 351 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/408/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 408 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/430/#laws.0.25.0">1977 c 430 s 25</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/649/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 649 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/355/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 355 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/509/#laws.0.143.0">1980 c 509 s 143</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/531/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 531 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/492/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 492 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/526/#laws.2.16.0">1982 c 526 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/276/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 276 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.198.0">1983 c 301 s 198</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/608/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 608 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 9 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/23/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/129/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 129 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/660/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 660 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/280/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 280 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/567/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 567 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/527/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 527 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 22 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.12.2.0">1997 c 239 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 291 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.39"><h1 class="shn">363A.39 AFFIRMATIVE MARKETING REGULATIONS.</h1><p>To promote and encourage open housing policies, the commissioner must establish affirmative marketing regulations for housing developers that receive more than $50,000 in state or local funds. The regulations must require the management or marketing agency for the housing development to adopt an information distribution or marketing plan for actively informing minorities and other protected groups of available housing opportunities. For purposes of this section, "protected groups" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.02#stat.43A.02.33">43A.02, subdivision 33</a>. The commissioner may adopt rules to carry out the purposes of this section.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.39.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/328/#laws.3.1.0">1989 c 328 art 3 s 1</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.40"><h1 class="shn">363A.40 RENTAL HOUSING PRIORITY; ACCESSIBLE UNITS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.40.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.40.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p class="in">(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p><p class="in">(b) "Accessible unit" means an accessible rental housing unit that meets the persons with disabilities requirements of the State Building Code.</p><p class="in">(c) "Landlord" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/504B.001#stat.504B.001.7">504B.001, subdivision 7</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.40.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.40.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Priority requirement.</span></h2><p>(a) A landlord of rental housing that contains accessible units must give priority for the rental of an accessible unit to a disabled person or a family with a disabled family member who will reside in the unit. The landlord must inform nondisabled persons and families that do not include a disabled family member of the possibility of being offered a non-disability-equipped unit as provided under this section before a rental agreement to rent an accessible unit is entered.</p><p>(b) If a nondisabled person or a family that does not include a disabled person is living in an accessible unit, the person or family must be offered a non-disability-equipped unit if the following conditions occur:</p><p>(1) a disabled person or a family with a disabled family member who will reside in the unit has signed a rental agreement to rent the accessible unit; and</p><p>(2) a similar non-disability-equipped unit in the same rental housing complex is available at the same rent.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.40.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/328/#laws.3.2.0">1989 c 328 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/199/#laws.2.14.0">1999 c 199 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/140/#laws.12.13.0">2007 c 140 art 12 s 13</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.41"><h1 class="shn">363A.41 CRIMINAL CODE; EFFECT.</h1><p>Nothing in this chapter alters the provisions of chapter 609 or other law relating to criminal penalties.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.41.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/22/#laws.0.20.0">1993 c 22 s 20</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.42"><h1 class="shn">363A.42 PUBLIC RECORDS; ACCESSIBILITY.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.42.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.42.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>For purposes of this section, "records" means any publicly available recorded information that is collected, created, received, maintained or disseminated by the executive, judicial or legislative branches of the state, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, the University of Minnesota, cities, towns, counties, school districts and all other political subdivisions of the state, regardless of physical form or method of storage.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.42.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.42.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Accessibility.</span></h2><p>Upon request by an individual, records must be made available within a reasonable time period to persons with disabilities in a manner consistent with state and federal laws prohibiting discrimination against persons with disabilities. Reasonable modifications must be made in any policies, practices and procedures that might otherwise deny equal access to records to individuals with disabilities.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.42.2a"><a title="Link to Subd. 2a." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.42.2a">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2><p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary except Laws 2009, chapter 131, this section does not apply to: (1) technology procured or developed prior to January 1, 2013, unless substantially modified or substantially enhanced after January 1, 2013; or (2) records that cannot be reasonably modified to be accessible without an undue burden as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.015#stat.16E.015.4">16E.015, subdivision 4</a>, to the public entity.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.42.3"><a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.42.3">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2><p>Violation of this section is subject to a penalty of $500 per violation, plus reasonable attorney fees, costs and disbursements, payable to a qualified disabled person under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.03#stat.363A.03.36">363A.03, subdivision 36</a>, who sought the accessible record under subdivision 2, by the public entity in violation of this section. The total amount of penalties payable to any individual or class regardless of the number of violations is limited to $15,000. In any class action or series of class actions which arise from a violation of this section, the amount of attorney fees awarded against the violating public entity may not exceed $15,000. Any action must be commenced within one year of the occurrence of the alleged violation.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.42.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/271/#laws.0.2.0">2010 c 271 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/347/#laws.1.22.0">2010 c 347 art 1 s 22</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.43"><h1 class="shn">363A.43 CONTINUING EDUCATION; ACCESSIBILITY.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.43.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.43.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Accessibility.</span></h2><p>Upon request by an individual, any continuing education or professional development course, offering, material or activity approved or administered by the state, political subdivisions of the state, the University of Minnesota or the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, must be made available within a reasonable time period to persons with disabilities in a manner consistent with state and federal laws prohibiting discrimination against persons with disabilities. Reasonable modifications must be made in any policies, practices and procedures that might otherwise deny equal access to continuing education or professional development to individuals with disabilities.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.43.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.43.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2><p>Violation of this section is subject to a penalty of $500 per violation, plus reasonable attorney fees, costs and disbursements, payable to a qualified disabled person under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.03#stat.363A.03.36">363A.03, subdivision 36</a>, who sought the accessible format under subdivision 1, by the public entity or the entity offering the course, material, or activity under a contract with a public entity. The total amount of penalties payable to any individual or class regardless of the number of violations is limited to $15,000. In any class action or series of class actions which arise from a violation of this section, the amount of attorney fees awarded against the violating public entity may not exceed $15,000. Any action must be commenced within one year of the occurrence of the alleged violation.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.363A.43.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/271/#laws.0.3.0">2010 c 271 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/347/#laws.1.23.0">2010 c 347 art 1 s 23</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.363A.44"><h1 class="shn">363A.44 EQUAL PAY CERTIFICATE.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.44.1"><a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.44.1">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2><p class="in">(a) No department, agency of the state, the Metropolitan Council, or an agency subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.143#stat.473.143.1">473.143, subdivision 1</a>, shall execute a contract for goods or services or an agreement for goods or services in excess of $500,000 with a business that has 40 or more full-time employees in this state or a state where the business has its primary place of business on a single day during the prior 12 months, unless the business has an equal pay certificate or it has certified in writing that it is exempt. A certificate is valid for four years.</p><p>(b) An agency that uses state money to pay for part or all of a capital project is subject to and must comply with the restrictions in this section for contracts exceeding $500,000. A political subdivision that uses state money to pay for part or all of a capital project is subject to and must comply with the restrictions in this section for contracts exceeding $1,000,000. For purposes of this paragraph, "agency," "political subdivision," "capital project," and "state money" have the meanings given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.36#stat.363A.36.1a">363A.36, subdivision 1a</a>. This paragraph applies to a capital project or discrete phase of a capital project for which state money has been appropriated on or after January 1, 2022.</p><p class="in">(c) This section does not apply to a business with respect to a specific contract if the commissioner of administration determines that application of this section would cause undue hardship to the contracting entity. This section does not apply to a contract to provide goods and services to individuals under chapters 43A, 62A, 62C, 62D, 62E, 256B, 256I, 256L, and 268A, with a business that has a license, certification, registration, provider agreement, or provider enrollment contract that is prerequisite to providing those goods and services. This section does not apply to contracts entered into by the State Board of Investment for investment options under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.965#stat.352.965.4">352.965, subdivision 4</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.363A.44.2"><a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/363A/full#stat.363A.44.2">§</a><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2><p class="in">(a) A business shall apply for an equal pay certificate by paying a $250 filing fee and submitting an equal pay compliance statement to the commissioner. The proceeds from the fees collected under this subdivision shall be deposited in an equal pay certificate special revenue account. Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes of this section. The commissioner shall issue an equal pay certificate of compliance to a business that submits to the commissioner a statement signed by the chairperson of the board or chief executive officer of the business:</p><p class="in">(1) that the business is in compliance with Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, Equal Pay Act of 1963, Minnesota Human Rights Act, and Minnesota Equal Pay for Equal Work Law;</p><p class="in">(2) that the average compensation for its female employees is not consistently below the average compensation for its male employees within each of the major job categories in the EEO-1 employee information report for which an employee is expected to perform work under the contract, taking into account factors such as length of service, requirements of specific jobs, experience, skill, effort, responsibility, working conditions of the job, or other mitigating factors;</p><p class="in">(3) that the business does not restrict employees of one sex to certain job classifications and makes retention and promotion decisions without regard to sex;</p><p class="in">(4) that wage and benefit disparities are corrected when identified to ensure compliance with the laws cited in clause (1) and with clause (2); and</p><p class="in">(5) how often wages and benefits are evaluated to ensure compliance with the laws cited in clause (1) and with clause (2).</p><p class="in">(b) The equal pay compliance statement shall also indicate whether the business, in setting compensation and benefits, utilizes:</p><p class="in">(1) a market pricing approach;</p><p class="in">(2) state prevailing wage or union contract requirements;</p><p class="in">(3) a performance pay system;</p><p class="in">(4) an internal analysis; or</p><p class="in">(5) an alternative approach to determine what level of wages and benefits to pay its employees.
